Function fft_corr_with_weights(a, w, n):
- len(a)=n, len(w)=2n-1.
- Need g_i = ∑_{k=0}^{2n-2} w_k a_{k-i} if 0≤k-i<n.
Equivalent convolution of w with reversed a at indices n-1+i.
Let ar = a[::-1]. conv = np.convolve(w, ar) or FFT. Need length len(w)+n-1=3n-2. FFT size power of two >=3n-2.
A = fft(ar, N); W=fft(w, N); conv = real(ifft(A*W)); g=2*conv[n-1:n-1+n].
But N=32768 for n=8192, okay. However convolution of w and ar; w length 16383, ar 8192 -> out length 24574. N=32768. Could use scipy.fft.fft. FFT better for moderate.

Let's design robust smooth multiplicative algorithm in detail.

Normalization: We can keep a sum exactly S. But objective scale invariant; if using mirror descent, a = np.exp(loga - logsum) * S. Using loga may improve. Initialize loga = log(a+eps) or log(a) with very small. Update loga ← loga - η grad (optionally center), then re-normalize via log-sum-exp: a_scale = S * exp(loga - maxlog) / sum(exp(loga-maxlog)). This prevents underflow and keeps nonnegative. It can create very wide dynamic range; may be okay. We can clip loga to max-min 50 to avoid degeneracy? If optimizer drives zeros, maybe okay. But too many zeros can be bad. Could add L2/entropy regularization? We can include a small prior: minimize H_p + λ * variance? Or keep elements not zero by adding tiny constant before log? Nonnegative includes zero, but strictly positive may help smooth. We can after optimizing allow zeros.

If using log variables, gradient of H wrt a times a gives gradient wrt loga: ∂H/∂loga_i = a_i ∂H/∂a_i. Multiplicative weights with gradient wrt a? Mirror descent on H(a) with KL constraint gives update loga_i ← loga_i - η ∂H/∂a_i. That's what we do. Using a_i∂H/∂a_i would be natural gradient? Need choose. Previous mirror used grad = ∂max/∂a (not multiplied by a) and update a*exp(-η grad). That is mirror descent with Euclidean? Actually multiplicative weights for minimizing linear objective with KL uses grad (coefficient) not a*grad. For nonconvex quadratic, either. Maybe using natural gradient a_i*grad leads to relative changes and better? Let's see: If a_i small, a_i*grad smaller, less reduction? If high partners involve small a_i? The subgradient w.r.t a_i indicates amount to reduce a_i. Multiplicative update a_i exp(-η grad_i) reduces proportional to grad_i independent of a_i. Natural gradient a_i exp(-η a_i grad_i) reduces proportional to a_i grad_i (local change). Which is better? For optimizing scale-invariant, maybe use grad wrt loga = a_i ∂H/∂a_i. Because changing loga is multiplicative. We can experiment conceptually. For max c, if one coordinate pairs with high active partners, ∂c/∂a_i=2 partner. If a_i large, natural force ∝ a_i*partner = 2 a_i partner, proportional to current product contribution. That seems appropriate: reduce coordinates contributing most to c. `_corr`:
```
def _corr_weights(a, w, N):
    ar = a[::-1]
    conv = irfft(rfft(ar, N) * rfft(w, N), N)
    n = len(a)
    return 2.0 * conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```

i=1: k=1 a0? k-i=0 valid =>w1 a0; k=2 a1=>w2 a1. g1=2(w1 a0+w2 a1).
Our conv indices n-1=1: conv[1]=w1 a1 + w0 a0 -> matches i=0? w0 a0+w1 a1 yes. conv[2]=w2 a1+w1 a0 -> matches i=1. Good. So slice [n-1:n+n-1]. Good.

Now initializations. We can write a function `generate_inits(rng)` returning list of arrays for a chosen n? Maybe we need multiple lengths. Let's define:
```
def make_basic_inits(n, rng, provided=None):
   inits=[]
   if provided is not None and len= n: inits.append(clip(provided))
   # random positive lognormal
   for k in range(3): inits.append(_normalize(rng.lognormal(0,1,n)))
   # random sine anti
   for phase in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi/4, -np.pi/2]:
      f=1+0.9*np.sin(2*np.pi*np.arange(n)/n + phase)
      f=np.maximum(f,1e-6)
      inits.append(f)
   # exponential
   for lam in [0.5,1,2]:
      x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
      inits.append(np.exp(lam*x))
   # block step signs
   for blocks in [2,3,5]:
      s=np.zeros(n)
      seg=n//blocks
      levels = rng.permutation(np.concatenate([np.ones(seg), np.ones(blocks-seg)*-1]?))
      ...
   # anti-palindromic random: first half z, second -reverse? For even n.
   if n%2==0: z=rng.normal(0,1,n//2); s=np.concatenate([z, -z[::-1]]?); a=1+0.8*s/np.max(abs(s)); positive.
   return normalized arrays (sum arbitrary)
```

Let's think about the SOTA LP approach; maybe implement a simplified linearized direction that can improve local. At current a, to reduce active lags, we can solve for delta in simplex: minimize maximum of linearized active lags. A simple choice: delta = α( e_i - a/n? ), where i is coordinate that reduces active sum most. Frank-Wolfe did that but toward all mass at one coordinate (bad). Instead, we can move a small amount equalizing active gradients: The optimal linearized step to reduce a weighted sum of active lags is along -gradient projected to sum zero, i.e., subtract mean. Mirror gradient does that in log domain. For direct a, update a ← a - η grad + correction to keep sum. That's Adam projection. Mirror natural may be better.

Potential problem: Natural gradient a*grad_a = 2 a_i ∑w a_{k-i}. If a_i and partner are both high product, force large. This directly reduces high products. Good.

Let's estimate mirror step effect: Suppose one active lag k with w=1, high product a_i a_{k-i}. g_i=2 a_i a_{k-i}, g_{k-i}=2 a_{k-i} a_i. Natural? Wait grad_a_i=2 a_{k-i}. Multiply by a_i gives 2 a_i a_{k-i}. Both endpoints get same force proportional to product. Reducing log of both equally: a_i ← a_i e^{-η*2a_i a_{k-i}}, product reduces. Good. If product large, step max scaled. Nice. This should reduce peak products. It also may reduce endpoints symmetrically, maintaining sum.

Using g centered by mean could add small increases to other coords to keep log-sum? Normalization already keeps sum; centering just removes common factor, equivalent to normalization. We can skip centering because log normalization removes constant. But for max normalization, if g all positive, normalization scales all down and then sum restored; relative unchanged. Centering not necessary. It can reduce range? It changes relative by subtracting mean; same as exp(-ηg)*normalize. Since normalization removes constant, exp(-η(g-mean)) yields same relative factors. So centering optional. Do it for numerical.

If g has negative values (natural gradient always nonnegative? grad_a_i =2∑w a_{k-i} with w,a nonnegative => grad_a≥0. Natural g=a*grad_a≥0. So no negative! Centering makes most negative, a few positive. Without centering, loga -= positive for all, then normalization increases all relative? Wait exp(-ηg) all decreased; normalization divides by sum, equivalent to relative changes; coordinates with smaller g decrease less. This is same as centering? If all g positive, exp(-η(g-mean)) = exp(-ηg)*exp(ηmean); sum normalization removes exp(ηmean). So yes same. We can skip centering but then gmax = max g. Update loga -= step*(g/gmax). All loga decrease by at least 0, max decrease step. Normalization restores sum. Good. But if g has zero for many, those unchanged relative. Fine.

Since g nonnegative, no negative forces: mirror never increases any coordinate relative to current? It can only decrease some relative. But normalization increases tiny coordinate? Actually relative coordinates with zero g increase relative to those with positive g, so yes some increase. No absolute increase before normalization. This monotonic relative shift may be good.

What if optimal requires increasing some high-lag coordinate? The gradient for max only decreases active products; since scaling invariant, we can scale all after. To reduce max, we never need increase any coordinate in relative terms? Maybe if a coordinate is too low causing other lags high elsewhere? But gradient active weights may make it partner, not increase. If a coordinate is small but paired with high variables, decreasing the high partner solves. Since all c_k increase with any a_i (positive partials), to reduce max you only decrease some coordinates; no need to increase any, because scaling can adjust total. If a coordinate is too low causing other coefficients below max but not lowering active, decreasing it further not needed; but increasing it would increase some c, likely not helpful. However, could increasing a coordinate reduce max by changed distribution? c_k are all positive monotone in a_i; increasing any a_i increases every coefficient? It increases c_k for k=i+j. It never decreases any c. So any increase of an element (with others fixed) cannot reduce any c. But since normalization keeps sum, increasing one requires decreasing others; the direction may increase a coordinate relative. ```
def _fast_score(a, S):
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000).astype(np.float64)
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return np.inf
    a=a*(S/s)
    c=_fft_self_conv(a)
    return float(c.max())
```

Define:
```
def _fft_self_conv_fixed(a, N):
   size=len(a)*2-1
   return np.real(irfft(rfft(a,N)**2,N))[:size]
```
Use `irfft` returns real already? `irfft` returns real ndarray. Good.
```
def _fft_corr_fixed(a,w,N):
   n=len(a)
   ar=a[::-1]
   conv=irfft(rfft(ar,N)*rfft(w,N), N)
   return 2.0*conv[n-1:n+ n -1]
```
Note `n-1:n-1+n` length n.

Normalize log sum function:
```
def _normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S):
    loga=np.asarray(loga)
    cap=np.log(999.0)
    floor=np.log(max(S/len(loga)*1e-10, 1e-300))
    loga=np.clip(loga, floor, cap)
    m=loga.max()
    sh=np.exp(loga-m)
    s=sh.sum()
    if s<=0:
       a=np.full(len(loga), S/len(loga)); return np.log(a)
    loga = loga - (m + np.log(s) - np.log(S))
    # after subtract, values might exceed cap? subtract constant max becomes log(S/s*exp(0)) maybe could >cap if one dominates. If one element close S, log ~log S=4.86<6.9, okay.
    return loga
```
If one element dominates, log sum normalization gives log S for that, <1000. Good.

Need ensure `a = np.exp(loga)` after normalization; sum exactly S within numerical.

Now, optimizer code details.

```
def run_smooth_mirror(a_init, n, max_time, rng, beta_start=2.0, beta_max=800.0, step_init=0.12):
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    N=next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    a=_normalize_sum(a_init,S)
    if a is None: a=np.ones(n)/n*S
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
    loga=_normalize_log_to_sum(loga,S)
    a=np.exp(loga)
    c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N)
    cur=float(c.max())
    best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy()
    beta=beta_start; step=step_init
    t=0; no_improve=0; restarts=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-start < max_time:
       # if time left small break
       if cur < best_score: best... no_improve=0
       # weights
       cmax=c.max()
       z=c-cmax
       z *= beta
       z = np.where(z < -700, -700, z)
       w=np.exp(z)
       wsum=w.sum()
       if wsum <= 0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
           w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
       else:
           w /= wsum
       grad_a=_fft_corr_fixed(a,w,N)
       g=a*grad_a
       # Since positive, maybe subtract median? use mean
       gmean=float(np.mean(g))
       g = g - gmean
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if gmax <= 1e-14: break
       g = g / gmax
       loga = loga - step*g
       loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S)
       a = np.exp(loga)
       c = _fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N)
       new_cur=float(c.max())
       if new_cur < cur:
           step = min(0.9, step*1.03)
           no_improve=0
       else:
           step = max(0.01, step*0.96)
           no_improve += 1
       cur = new_cur
       if cur < best_score:
           best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
       t += 1
       if t % 100 == 0:
          beta = min(beta_max, beta*1.07)
       if no_improve > 1500 and restarts < 8:
          noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n)
          loga = loga + noise
          loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga,S)
          a=np.exp(loga)
          c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N); cur=float(c.max())
          beta=max(2.0,beta*0.5); step=0.12; no_improve=0; restarts+=1
       if not np.isfinite(cur): # fallback
          break
    return best_a, best_score
```
Note we check `if cur < best_score` at top and after new; duplicate. Fine.

Potential issue: `g = a*grad_a` is nonnegative; after subtract mean, max abs roughly variance. If g is very flat, gmax small, step division can amplify noise? But if flat gradient, need no change; gmax small due noise. Use threshold 1e-10. Since normalized sum, a values ~0.015, grad_a typical? If w broad, grad_a ~2*avg a ~0.03, g~0.00045, variance small. gmax maybe 1e-4, not too small. Step update with g/gmax gives relative direction. Good.

But subtracting mean makes g zero-sum; if g is constant, gmax 0; no update. Good.

Beta schedule: Multiplying by 1.07 every 100 -> over 1000 steps factor 7.36, over 5000 factor 188. Max 800. Good.

def _as_normalized_sequence(init, n, S):
   a = np.asarray(init, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   if a.size != n:
      # interpolate or truncate?
      if a.size > n: a=a[:n]
      else: pad by tiling?
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12:
      a=np.ones(n)*S/n
   else: a=a*(S/s)
   return a
```

`_normalize_sum(a, S)`:
```
def _normalize_sum(a, S):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        a = np.full(a.size, 1.0)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a * (S / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    # renormalize after clip if needed
    s2 = a.sum()
    if s2 <= 1e-12:
        a = np.full(a.size, S / a.size)
    else:
        a = a * (S / s2)
    return a
```
If a size 0? not.

`_fast_loss(a)` maybe:
```
def _fast_loss(a):
    n=len(a); S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=_normalize_sum(a,S)
    N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    c=irfft(rfft(a,N)**2,N)
    return float(c[:2*n-1].max())
```

`_generate_inits(n, rng, provided)`:
```
x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
inits=[]
if provided and len==n: inits.append(provided.copy())
inits.append(rng.random(n)+1e-6)
inits.append(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5,n)))
# linear inc/dec
inits.append(1.0 + 2.0*x)
inits.append(1.0 + 2.0*(1.0-x))
# exponential
inits.append(np.exp(1.0*x))
inits.append(np.exp(2.0*x))
# sine
for m,phase in [(1,np.pi/2),(1,0),(2,np.pi/2),(3,0)]:
    f = 1.0 + 0.8*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x + phase)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-3)
    inits.append(f)
# anti random
if n%2==0:
    z = rng.normal(0,1,n//2)
    s = np.empty(n)
    s[:n//2]=z
    s[n//2:]= -z[::-1]
    scale=np.max(np.abs(s))+1e-9
    inits.append(np.maximum(1.0 + 0.9*(s/scale),1e-3))
# random two-level
s = rng.integers(0,2,n)*2-1
if s.mean()!=0: s = s - (2*s.mean()+1?) Better: adjust to zero mean: s = np.where(rng.random(n)<0.5,1,-1); a=1+0.6*s? mean not zero but okay nonneg.
inits.append(1.0 + 0.6*s)
# block gradient
for blocks in [3,5]:
   seg=n//blocks
   levels = np.linspace(0.2,2.0,blocks)
   perm = rng.permutation(levels)
   arr=np.repeat(perm, seg)
   if len(arr)<n: arr=np.concatenate([arr, np.full(n-len(arr), 1.0)])
   inits.append(arr)
return inits
```
Need avoid lambdas. `rng.integers` okay.

Scoring inits:
```
def _score_inits(inits, n):
    scored=[]
    for a in inits:
       S=np.sqrt(2*n)
       aa=_normalize_sum(a,n*S?) no, S.
       N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
       c=irfft(rfft(aa,N)**2,N)
       v=float(c[:2*n-1].max())
       if np.isfinite(v): scored.append((v, aa))
    scored.sort(key=_score_item)
    return scored
```

Let's write `run_smooth_mirror` carefully to avoid modifying input.

Pseudo code in final:
```
def run_smooth_mirror(a_init, n, max_time, rng, beta_start=2.0, beta_max=800.0, step_init=0.15, allow_restart=True):
    if max_time <= 0: return a_init,_fast_loss(a_init)
    S = np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a = _normalize_sum(a_init, S)
    N = next_fast_len(3*n - 2)
    tiny_floor = max(1e-300, (S/n)*1e-12)
    tiny_log = np.log(tiny_floor)
    loga = np.log(a + 0.0) # if zeros give -inf
    loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, tiny_log)
    loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S, n)
    a = np.exp(loga)
    c = _fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N)
    cur = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0
    best_score = cur if np.isfinite(cur) else np.inf
    best_a = a.copy()
    beta = float(beta_start); step = float(step_init)
    t=0; no_improve=0; restarts=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
       if not np.isfinite(cur): break
       if cur < best_score:
          best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
       # if not enough time, break
       rem = max_time - (time.monotonic()-start)
       if rem < 0.05: break
       # weights
       cmax = float(c.max())
       z = c - cmax
       z *= beta
       np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0, out=z)
       w = np.exp(z)
       wsum = float(w.sum())
       if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 1e-300:
          w = np.zeros_like(c)
          w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
       else:
          w /= wsum
       grad = _fft_corr_fixed(a, w, N)
       # residual
       g = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
       gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-14:
          break
       g /= gmax
       loga = loga - step * g
       loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S, n)
       a = np.exp(loga)
       c = _fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N)
       new_cur = float(c.max())
       if np.isfinite(new_cur):
          cur = new_cur
       else:
          break
       if cur < best_score:
          best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
       if cur < float(c_hist?) no, use previous cur
```
Need previous cur before update to adapt step. Save `old_cur = cur` before update. Then after new:
```
if new_cur < old_cur:
   step = min(1.0, step*1.03); no_improve=0
else:
   step = max(0.015, step*0.97); no_improve += 1
```

    logr = np.log(rnd)
    loga = 0.5*loga + 0.5*logr
    loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga,S,n)
    a=np.exp(loga)

```

Now, `_normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S, n)` uses n. It should enforce cap/floor.
```
def _normalize_log_to_sum(loga, S, n):
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    max_log = np.log(999.0)
    base = max(S / float(n), 1e-300)
    min_log = np.log(base) - 40.0
    np.clip(loga, min_log, max_log, out=loga)
    m = float(np.max(loga))
    shifted = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = float(shifted.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, np.log(S / n))
    loga -= (m + np.log(s) - np.log(S))
    # after subtract, could be below min; clip and renorm one more time? maybe not.
    np.clip(loga, min_log, max_log, out=loga)
    return loga
```
If clipping after subtract changes sum; but rare. Could loop once:
```
while True:
  clip; m; shifted; s; loga -= ...; if max change small break
```
Keep simple: after subtract, re-normalize without clip? If we clip again, sum may off. We can do:
```
# rescale to exact sum without clipping first
m = loga.max(); sh=exp(loga-m); s=sh.sum(); loga = loga - (m+log(s)-log(S))
# then if any exceeds cap or below min, adjust and renorm again
if loga.max() > max_log or loga.min() < min_log:
    np.clip(loga,min_log,max_log,out=loga)
    m=...; loga=...
return loga
```
This maintains sum unless cap hits. If cap hits, sum may not be S; then final a can be renormalized in `_exp_log`. But convolution should have sum S. We can in optimizer after `a=np.exp(loga)` call `_normalize_sum(a,S)` to enforce. That may be safer and handles cap. But doing full normalize_sum each iter (clip, sum, scale) is cheap. Then loga can be updated from a? We can just set `a = _normalize_sum(np.exp(loga), S)` and not require loga exact sum. But next loga update uses loga not matching a if we renorm a. We should update loga after renormalizing a: `loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))`? That loses relative range? We can do:
- After update and normalization via log, set `a = np.exp(loga)`.
- Enforce a sum and clip: `a = _normalize_sum(a, S)`.
- Update loga to log a (with floor) for consistency: `loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny_floor))`.
This ensures a and loga match, sum exact. Extra log/exp each iteration but okay. It may squash dynamic range due floor/cap. Good.

So `_normalize_log_to_sum` only for relative; final consistency via a. We can combine:
```
def _log_to_a(loga, S, n):
   loga = _normalize_log_to_sum(loga,S,n)
   a = np.exp(loga)
   a = _normalize_sum(a,S)
   tiny = max(S/n*1e-12, 1e-300)
   loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
   return a, loga
```
But if _normalize_sum clips/cap, loga updated. We can use this in optimizer after each update. This adds log and clip but okay.

In initial, `a=_normalize_sum`; `loga=np.log(a+floor)`; no need log normalize.

In `_log_to_a`, if many values floor, sum maybe; _normalize_sum handles. Use `a = np.exp(loga - loga.max())` in normalize_log then scale? But okay.

Let's simplify:
```
def _a_from_log(loga, S, n):
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64)
    max_log = np.log(999.0)
    tiny = max(S / float(n) * 1e-10, 1e-300)
    min_log = np.log(tiny)
    np.clip(loga, min_log, max_log, out=loga)
    m = loga.max(); sh=np.exp(loga-m); s=sh.sum()
    if s <= 0: a=np.full(n,S/n)
    else: a = (S/s)*np.exp(loga-m)
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s2=a.sum()
    if s2<=0: a=np.full(n,S/n)
    else: a = a*(S/s2)
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
    return a, loga
```
This ensures a sum S within rounding. Good.

Use `tiny = max(S/n*1e-10, 1e-300)`. For n=8192 S/n=0.0156, tiny=1.56e-12, log -27. For floor too high? e-12 relative 1e-10 of mean, negligible. Good.

In optimizer, initial loga = np.log(max(a,tiny)). After update, call `a, loga = _a_from_log(loga, S, n)`. We don't need separate `_normalize_log_to_sum`.

Need ensure `loga` length n. In restart blend, `loga = 0.7*loga + 0.3*np.log(rnd+tiny)` then `_a_from_log`.

Pseudo final optimizer:
```
def run_smooth_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta_start=2.0, beta_max=800.0, step_init=0.15, allow_restart=True):
   a0=np.asarray(a_init,dtype=float).copy()
   n=len(a0)
   S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
   a=_normalize_sum(a_init,S)
   tiny=max(S/n*1e-10,1e-300)
   loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,tiny))
   N=next_fast_len(3*n-2)
   c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N); cur=float(c.max())
   best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy()
   beta=beta_start; step=step_init; t=0; no_improve=0; restarts=0
   start=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic()-start < max_time:
       if cur < best_score: best...
       old_cur=cur
       cmax=float(c.max())
       z=c-cmax; z*=beta; np.clip(z,-700,0,out=z); w=np.exp(z); wsum=w.sum()
       if wsum<=...: one hot
       else: w/=wsum
       grad=_fft_corr_fixed(a,w,N)
       g=grad-float(np.mean(grad))
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
       if gmax<=1e-14: break
       g/=gmax
       loga=loga-step*g
       a, loga = _a_from_log(loga,S,n)
       c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N); new_cur=float(c.max())
       if not finite: break
       if new_cur < old_cur:
          step=min(1.0, step*1.03)
          no_improve=0
       else:
          step=max(0.015, step*0.97)
          no_improve +=1
       cur=new_cur
       if cur < best_score: best...
       t+=1
       if t%100==0:
          beta=min(beta_max, beta*1.06)
          if t%2000==0: print(...)
       if allow_restart and no_improve>2000 and restarts<8:
          rnd=_normalize_sum(rng.random(n),S)
          logr=np.log(np.maximum(rnd,tiny))
          loga=0.7*loga+0.3*logr
          a,loga=_a_from_log(loga,S,n)
          c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N); cur=float(c.max())
          beta=max(2.0,beta*0.4); step=0.2; no_improve=0; restarts+=1
   return best_a, best_score
```
We compute `if cur < best_score` at loop start and after new; can combine: at top after new? Use only after new and initial. At loop start, cur from previous new already considered. But if best updated in previous, fine. We'll just check after updating and at start for initial.

Potential issue: `z*=beta` modifies `z` view? `z=c-cmax` new array, okay. `np.clip(z,...,out=z)` okay. `w=np.exp(z)` new. c remains.

`_fft_self_conv_fixed` returns length 2n-1. With N maybe 3n, okay.

Let's define `_score_inits` to avoid recomputing S each time.
```
def _score_inits(inits, n):
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    scored=[]
    for init in inits:
       a=_normalize_sum(init,S)
       c=_fft_self_conv_fixed(a,N)
       v=float(c.max())
       if np.isfinite(v): scored.append((v,a))
    if scored: scored.sort(key=_score_item)
    return scored
```
But `_fft_self_conv_fixed` needs N and returns length 2n-1; okay.

Let's think about mathematical lower bound and if our algorithm can break 1.5027. The mirror method from provided may improve but maybe not enough. Could we use the provided sequence and exploit its structure to construct a better one via simple transformations? For example, if a has length 8192, we can apply a "mutualization": For each pair (i,j=n-1-i), set product low by transferring mass from higher to lower. Mirror does. But we can directly optimize all mirror pairs to reduce central lag: For central lag, the contribution is sum a_i a_j. Given total sum of each pair maybe not fixed (mass can move between pairs). def _fft_corr_from_fft(a, w, N, A=None):
    n=len(a)
    if A is None: A=rfft(a,N)
    W=rfft(w,N)
    corr=irfft(np.conj(W)*A, N)
    if n==1:
       return 2.0*corr[0:1]
    out=np.empty(n)
    out[0]=corr[0]
    out[1:]=corr[N-n+1:N][::-1]
    return 2.0*out
```

Function:

def _self_conv_and_fft(a, N):
    A = rfft(a, N)
    c = irfft(A * A, N)
    size = 2*len(a)-1
    c = np.maximum(c[:size], 0.0)
    return c, A

`_corr_from_fft(w,N,A,n)`:
```
def _corr_from_fft(w, N, A, n):
    W = rfft(w, N)
    corr = irfft(np.conj(W) * A, N)
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out[0] = corr[0]
    if n > 1:
        out[1:] = corr[N - n + 1:N][::-1]
    return 2.0 * out
```

Use `g = grad - np.median(grad)`? For stationarity constant, mean constant average equals average of grad; but normalization sum constraint Lagrange μ should be weighted? For fixed sum, μ may equal average of grad? Since ∑δ=0, we can subtract any constant; mean is convenient. Median may better if outliers. But constant c in update doesn't matter; subtracting median vs mean changes relative because not a constant? Actually subtracting any constant yields same relative factors after normalization! Wait `exp(loga - step*(g - C))` normalized = `exp(loga - step*g)` for any constant C, because exp(step*C) common. So subtraction constant is irrelevant; mean vs median does not change relative factors. Correction: If we do g = grad - C, then update loga -= step*(grad - C) = loga - step*grad + step*C. The constant step*C is common and removed by normalization. So C arbitrary. Thus mean/median no effect relative. Good; we can just use grad directly and normalize by gmax, then _a_from_log normalizes; subtract mean optional numerical to reduce range? Since _a_from_log also normalizes, subtracting mean could reduce exp overflow. We can subtract `float(np.max(grad))`? That also constant, but if we then gmax = max(abs(g)), normalizing by max abs changes? Wait scaling by gmax matters! If we subtract C then divide by gmax, compared to not subtracting, the direction shape changes? Adding constant then normalizing by max abs is not invariant because normalization constant changes? In log update, `step * (g/gmax)`. If g = grad - C, and gmax normalized, the shape is not same as grad/gmax_grad, because constant subtraction before max-normalization changes relative scaling. But mathematically update with constant C before scaling not equivalent: constants in exponent that are not uniform after dividing by gmax? Dividing by gmax is a scalar; adding C then dividing by another scalar is not just adding uniform constant; it changes the offset relative to the scale. However, the direction of multiplicative update depends on the gradients up to adding a constant? For multiplicative update with step η, `a_i exp(-η grad_i)` normalized is invariant to adding any constant to grad: exp(-η(grad_i+C)) = exp(-ηC) exp(-ηgrad_i), common factor. But if we also scale gradient by a factor depending on C, the effective η changes but not the relative factors. Scaling all grad by positive factor just changes η, not relative factors. Adding a constant before scaling by maxabs: Let h_i=grad_i-C. Then update ∝ a_i exp(-step*h_i / max|h|) = a_i exp(- (step/max|h|) grad_i + constant). Scaling all grad by scalar 1/max|h|; constant has no effect. So relative factors same as using grad with a different step scalar. Good. Thus subtracting C before max normalization is equivalent to using grad directly with a different step (step/max|h| instead of step/max|grad|). Wait h = grad - C; h/gmax_h = (grad - C)/gmax_h = grad/(gmax_h) - constant/gmax_h. Multiplying by step gives step/gmax_h * grad - constant. Constant removed, scaling step/gmax_h. So indeed just different step. But gmax_h may be smaller/larger. The relative factors same as grad normalized by its max? Not exactly because h/gmax_h = grad/gmax_h + const. The coefficient of grad is 1/gmax_h, while if using grad normalized by max|grad| coefficient is 1/max|grad|. Since step adapts to maxlog, only step magnitude differs. So subtracting C before max normalization is fine; it affects effective step but not direction (shape) because grad direction under multiplicative up to constant? Actually gradient direction in multiplicative update is determined by grad_i modulo addition of constant and positive scaling; any transformation `a*grad + b` with a>0 gives same relative factors (b constant). So h = grad - C is via a=1,b=-C. Thus direction same. Good. So subtracting mean only changes effective max normalization maybe, but we adapt step. Fine.

Thus we can simply use `g=grad`; `gmax=np.max(g)` (since nonnegative); `g=g/gmax`; update. This makes max log change step. No need mean. But if grad has zeros, okay. If grad all equal, g=uniform/gmax, update uniform, normalization no change. If gmax small, okay. Use nonnegative max rather than abs. This avoids noise. Let's do:
```
g = grad
gmax = float(np.max(g))
if gmax <= 1e-14: break
g = g / gmax
loga -= step * g
```
Since grad nonnegative (up to numerical), no center. This is simpler. But _a_from_log normalizes, constant effects handled. This may be good because partial derivatives nonnegative. Do `g = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)`. Then gmax. This enforces monotone decrease relative. Good.

```
def _generate_inits(n, rng, provided=None, many=True):
    inits=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    base=None
    if provided is not None:
       arr=np.asarray(provided,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
       if arr.size==n and np.sum(np.clip(arr,0,1000))>0:
           base=_normalize_sum(arr, np.sqrt(2.0*n))
           inits.append(base.copy())
    if many:
       inits.append(np.maximum(rng.random(n),1e-6))
       inits.append(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.5,n)))
       inits.append(1.0 + 2.0*x)
       inits.append(1.0 + 2.0*(1.0-x))
       inits.append(np.exp(1.0*x))
       inits.append(np.exp(2.0*x))
       for m, phase in ((1, np.pi/2.0),(1,0.0),(2, np.pi/2.0),(2,0.0),(3,0.0)):
           f=1.0+0.9*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*m*x + phase)
           inits.append(np.maximum(f,1e-3))
       if n % 2 == 0:
           h=n//2
           z=rng.normal(0.0,1.0,h)
           s=np.empty(n)
           s[:h]=z
           s[h:]=-z[::-1]
           scale=float(np.max(np.abs(s)))
           if scale>0:
              inits.append(np.maximum(1.0+0.9*(s/scale),1e-3))
       s2=rng.integers(0,2,n)
       s2=2.0*s2-1.0
       inits.append(1.0 + 0.7*s2)
       for blocks in (3,5):
          levels=np.linspace(0.2,2.0,blocks)
          perm=rng.permutation(levels)
          arr=np.repeat(perm, n//blocks)
          if arr.size < n:
              arr=np.concatenate((arr, np.ones(n-arr.size)*1.0))
          inits.append(arr)
    else:
       inits.append(np.maximum(rng.random(n),1e-6))
       inits.append(1.0 + 0.9*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*x + np.pi/2.0))
       inits.append(np.exp(1.5*x))
    if base is not None:
       # transformations
       for alpha in (0.25,0.6,1.2):
          inits.append(np.maximum(base * np.exp(-alpha * base[::-1]),1e-12))
       for alpha in (0.25,0.6):
          inits.append(np.maximum(base * np.exp(-alpha * np.roll(base,1)),1e-12))
       inits.append(np.maximum(base + 0.1*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n),1e-12))
    # Ensure length exactly
    cleaned=[]
    for a in inits:
       arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
       if arr.size < n:
           arr=np.concatenate((arr, np.ones(n-arr.size, dtype=np.float64)))
       elif arr.size > n:
           arr=arr[:n]
       arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
       if arr.sum() > 1e-12:
           cleaned.append(arr)
       else:
           cleaned.append(np.ones(n))
    return cleaned
```
Note `rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)` for base noise can make negative, max. Good.

Potential issue: `np.roll(base,1)` wrap creates interaction edge; okay.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2350):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 60: budget=60.0
    budget = min(budget, 2380.0)
    deadline = start + budget - 5.0
    # provided detection
    ...
    polish_fraction=0.18
    polish_budget = max(30.0, polish_fraction*budget)
    explore_budget = budget - polish_budget - 10.0
    if explore_budget < 60: explore_budget = max(30,budget-polish_budget)
    # generate/score main
    main_inits = _generate_inits(main_len, rng, provided_use, many=True)
    main_scored = _score_inits(main_inits, main_len)
    # second
    second_len=...
    second_scored=[]
    if second_len != main_len and second_len >=1024:
       sec_inits = _generate_inits(second_len, rng, None, many=False)
       second_scored = _score_inits(sec_inits, second_len)
    tasks=[]
    if main_scored:
       tasks.append((0.36, main_scored[0][1], 'mirror', True))
       tasks.append((0.12, None, 'adam', False))
       if len(main_scored)>1: tasks.append((0.14, main_scored[1][1], 'mirror', True))
       if len(main_scored)>2: tasks.append((0.08, main_scored[2][1], 'mirror', True))
    if second_scored:
       tasks.append((0.14, second_scored[0][1], 'mirror', True))
       if len(second_scored)>1: tasks.append((0.06, second_scored[1][1], 'mirror', True))
    # If no tasks, fallback
    total_frac=sum(f for f,_,_,_ in tasks)
    if total_frac<=0:
       a=np.ones(main_len); tasks=[(1.0,a,'mirror',True)]; total_frac=1.0
    ...
    global_score=np.inf; global_a=None; results=[]
    for frac, init, method, use_best in tasks:
       time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if time_left < 5: break
       task_time = frac/total_frac * explore_budget
       if task_time < 5: continue
       if use_best and global_a is not None:
          a_use = global_a
       else:
          a_use = init if init is not None else (main_scored[0][1] if main_scored else np.ones(main_len))
       if method == 'mirror':
          a_best, score = run_smooth_mirror(a_use, task_time, rng, beta_start=2.0, beta_max=800.0, step_init=0.15, allow_restart=True)
       else:
          a_best, score = run_log_adam(a_use, task_time, rng, beta_start=5.0, beta_max=1200.0, lr_init=0.30, allow_restart=True)
       results.append((score,a_best))
       if score < global_score:
          global_score=score; global_a=a_best
    # polish
    time_left=deadline - time.monotonic()
    if global_a is not None and time_left > 5:
       a_best,score = run_smooth_mirror(global_a, time_left, rng, beta_start=40.0, beta_max=2000.0, step_init=0.05, allow_restart=False)
       if score < global_score: update
       else: a_best? keep global.
    # final choose: sort results? Actually global.
    final_a = global_a if global_a is not None else np.ones(main_len)
    final_a = np.clip(final_a,0,1000)
    if final_a.sum()<0.01: final_a=np.ones(1024)
    # verify
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in final_a])
    except Exception:
       val=np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val):
       # maybe try result with main_len
       for score,a in sorted(results,key=_ff_key)[:5]:
          if a.size <= 20000:
             try:
                v2=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in np.clip(a,0,1000)])
             except Exception: v2=np.inf
             if np.isfinite(v2): final_a=np.clip(a,0,1000); break
    return [float(x) for x in final_a]

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing

    2*n*max_k (a*a)[k] / (sum(a))^2

by working on the scale-invariant simplex sum(a) = sqrt(2*n), where the
objective equals max_k (a*a)[k].

The main optimizer is a log-domain multiplicative-weights / mirror-descent
method with an annealed softmax over the convolution coefficients.  A small
Adam-in-log-space variant and several constructive/random initializations are
also used.  FFT-based linear convolutions and correlations are used for all
inner iterations.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft, next_fast_len


def _score_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _normalize_sum(a, S):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a * (S / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 <= 1e-12:
        a = np.full(a.size, S / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = a * (S / s2)
    return a


def _a_from_log(loga, S, n):
    loga = np.asarray(loga, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    base = max(S / float(n), 1e-300)
    min_log = np.log(base) - 40.0
    max_log = np.log(999.0)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(loga)):
        loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, min_log)

    np.clip(loga, min_log, max_log, out=loga)

    m = float(np.max(loga))
    shifted = np.exp(loga - m)
    s = float(shifted.sum())

    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = (S / s) * shifted
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s2 = float(a.sum())
        if s2 <= 1e-300:
            a = np.full(n, S / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            a = a * (S / s2)

    tiny = max(S / float(n) * 1e-10, 1e-300)
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
    return a, loga


def _self_conv_and_fft(a, N):
    A = rfft(a, N)
    c = irfft(A * A, N)
    size = 2 * len(a) - 1
    c = np.maximum(c[:size], 0.0)
    return c, A


def _fft_self_conv_fixed(a, N):
    c, _ = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
    return c


def _corr_from_fft(w, N, A, n):
    """
    Return g_i = 2 * sum_k w_k a_{k-i}, where a has FFT A and w has FFT W,
    using zero-padded linear correlation.  N must be at least 2*n-1.
    """
    W = rfft(w, N)
    corr = irfft(np.conj(W) * A, N)
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out[0] = corr[0]
    if n > 1:
        out[1:] = corr[N - n + 1:N][::-1]
    return 2.0 * out


def _softmax_weights(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[0] = 1.0
        return w

    z = c - cmax
    z = z * beta
    np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0, out=z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())

    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = w / s
    return w


def _fast_loss(a):
    n = len(a)
    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a, S)
    N = next_fast_len(2 * n - 1)
    c = _fft_self_conv_fixed(a, N)
    return float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")


def _generate_inits(n, rng, provided=None, many=True):
    inits = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    base = None

    if provided is not None:
        arr = np.asarray(provided, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if arr.size == n:
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.sum() > 0.0:
                base = _normalize_sum(arr, S)
                inits.append(base.copy())

    if many:
        inits.append(np.maximum(rng.random(n), 1e-6))
        inits.append(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, n)))

        inits.append(1.0 + 2.0 * x)
        inits.append(1.0 + 2.0 * (1.0 - x))
        inits.append(np.exp(1.0 * x))
        inits.append(np.exp(2.0 * x))

        for m, phase in ((1, np.pi / 2.0), (1, 0.0), (2, np.pi / 2.0), (2, 0.0), (3, 0.0)):
            f = 1.0 + 0.9 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + phase)
            inits.append(np.maximum(f, 1e-3))

        if n % 2 == 0:
            h = n // 2
            z = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, h)
            s = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
            s[:h] = z
            s[h:] = -z[::-1]
            scale = float(np.max(np.abs(s)))
            if scale > 0.0:
                inits.append(np.maximum(1.0 + 0.9 * (s / scale), 1e-3))

        s2 = rng.integers(0, 2, n)
        s2 = 2.0 * s2 - 1.0
        inits.append(1.0 + 0.7 * s2)

        for blocks in (3, 5):
            levels = np.linspace(0.2, 2.0, blocks)
            perm = rng.permutation(levels)
            arr = np.repeat(perm, n // blocks)
            if arr.size < n:
                arr = np.concatenate((arr, np.ones(n - arr.size, dtype=np.float64)))
            elif arr.size > n:
                arr = arr[:n]
            inits.append(arr)
    else:
        inits.append(np.maximum(rng.random(n), 1e-6))
        inits.append(np.maximum(1.0 + 0.9 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x + np.pi / 2.0), 1e-3))
        inits.append(np.exp(1.5 * x))

    if base is not None:
        for alpha in (0.25, 0.6, 1.2):
            inits.append(np.maximum(base * np.exp(-alpha * base[::-1]), 1e-12))
        for alpha in (0.25, 0.6):
            inits.append(np.maximum(base * np.exp(-alpha * np.roll(base, 1)), 1e-12))
        inits.append(np.maximum(base + 0.1 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n), 1e-12))

    cleaned = []
    for a in inits:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if arr.size < n:
            arr = np.concatenate((arr, np.ones(n - arr.size, dtype=np.float64)))
        elif arr.size > n:
            arr = arr[:n]
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.sum() > 1e-12:
            cleaned.append(arr)
        else:
            cleaned.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    return cleaned


def _score_inits(inits, n):
    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    N = next_fast_len(2 * n - 1)
    scored = []
    for init in inits:
        a = _normalize_sum(init, S)
        c = _fft_self_conv_fixed(a, N)
        v = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(v):
            scored.append((v, a))
    scored.sort(key=_score_key)
    return scored


def run_smooth_mirror(a_init, max_time, rng, beta_start=2.0, beta_max=800.0,
                      step_init=0.15, allow_restart=True):
    a0 = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = len(a0)
    if n <= 0:
        return a0, float("inf")

    if max_time <= 0.0:
        return _normalize_sum(a0, np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))), _fast_loss(a0)

    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a0, S)
    tiny = max(S / float(n) * 1e-10, 1e-300)
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
    N = next_fast_len(2 * n - 1)

    c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
    cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
    best_score = cur
    best_a = a.copy()

    beta = float(beta_start)
    step = float(step_init)
    t = 0
    no_improve = 0
    restarts = 0
    start = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
        if not np.isfinite(cur):
            break

        old_cur = cur

        w = _softmax_weights(c, beta)
        grad = _corr_from_fft(w, N, A, n)
        grad = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)

        gmax = float(np.max(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-14:
            break

        grad = grad / gmax
        loga = loga - step * grad
        a, loga = _a_from_log(loga, S, n)

        c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
        new_cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
        if not np.isfinite(new_cur):
            break

        if new_cur < old_cur:
            step = min(1.0, step * 1.035)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            step = max(0.015, step * 0.965)
            no_improve += 1

        cur = new_cur
        if cur < best_score:
            best_score = cur
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0

        t += 1
        if t % 100 == 0:
            beta = min(beta_max, beta * 1.06)
        if t % 5000 == 0:
            print("mirror n=%d t=%d cur=%.7f best=%.7f beta=%.2f step=%.4f restarts=%d"
                  % (n, t, cur, best_score, beta, step, restarts))

        if allow_restart and no_improve > 2500 and restarts < 8:
            rnd = _normalize_sum(rng.random(n), S)
            logr = np.log(np.maximum(rnd, tiny))
            loga = 0.7 * loga + 0.3 * logr
            a, loga = _a_from_log(loga, S, n)
            c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
            cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
            if not np.isfinite(cur):
                break
            beta = max(2.0, beta * 0.35)
            step = 0.22
            no_improve = 0
            restarts += 1
            print("mirror restart n=%d cur=%.7f best=%.7f" % (n, cur, best_score))

    return best_a, best_score


def run_log_adam(a_init, max_time, rng, beta_start=5.0, beta_max=1200.0,
                 lr_init=0.30, allow_restart=True):
    a0 = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = len(a0)
    if n <= 0:
        return a0, float("inf")

    if max_time <= 0.0:
        return _normalize_sum(a0, np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))), _fast_loss(a0)

    S = np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a0, S)
    tiny = max(S / float(n) * 1e-10, 1e-300)
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, tiny))
    N = next_fast_len(2 * n - 1)

    c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
    cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
    best_score = cur
    best_a = a.copy()

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b1 = 0.90
    b2 = 0.99
    eps = 1e-8
    beta = float(beta_start)
    lr = float(lr_init)
    t = 0
    no_improve = 0
    restarts = 0
    start = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() - start < max_time:
        if not np.isfinite(cur):
            break

        old_cur = cur

        w = _softmax_weights(c, beta)
        grad = _corr_from_fft(w, N, A, n)
        grad = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)

        gmax = float(np.max(grad))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-14:
            break

        g = grad / gmax
        t += 1

        m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * g
        v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (g * g)
        mhat = m / (1.0 - b1 ** t)
        vhat = v / (1.0 - b2 ** t)
        step = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + eps)
        np.clip(step, -1.0, 1.0, out=step)

        loga = loga - step
        a, loga = _a_from_log(loga, S, n)

        c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
        new_cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
        if not np.isfinite(new_cur):
            break

        if new_cur < old_cur:
            lr = min(1.0, lr * 1.02)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            lr = max(0.02, lr * 0.975)
            no_improve += 1

        cur = new_cur
        if cur < best_score:
            best_score = cur
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0

        if t % 100 == 0:
            beta = min(beta_max, beta * 1.06)
        if t % 5000 == 0:
            print("adam   n=%d t=%d cur=%.7f best=%.7f beta=%.2f lr=%.4f restarts=%d"
                  % (n, t, cur, best_score, beta, lr, restarts))

        if allow_restart and no_improve > 2500 and restarts < 8:
            rnd = _normalize_sum(rng.random(n), S)
            logr = np.log(np.maximum(rnd, tiny))
            loga = 0.7 * loga + 0.3 * logr
            a, loga = _a_from_log(loga, S, n)
            c, A = _self_conv_and_fft(a, N)
            cur = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
            if not np.isfinite(cur):
                break
            beta = max(2.0, beta * 0.35)
            lr = 0.25
            m.fill(0.0)
            v.fill(0.0)
            t = 0
            no_improve = 0
            restarts += 1
            print("adam restart n=%d cur=%.7f best=%.7f" % (n, cur, best_score))

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    """Search for a best non-negative sequence under the time budget."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    raw_budget = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(raw_budget) or raw_budget <= 120.0:
        raw_budget = 120.0
    raw_budget = min(raw_budget, 2400.0)
    usable = max(120.0, raw_budget - 40.0)
    deadline = t0 + usable

    provided = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size >= 64 and arr.sum() > 0.0:
            provided = arr
    except Exception:
        provided = None

    if provided is not None and 1024 <= provided.size <= 16384:
        main_len = int(provided.size)
        provided_use = provided
    else:
        main_len = 8192
        provided_use = None

    second_len = main_len * 2
    if second_len > 16384 or second_len == main_len:
        second_len = main_len // 2
        if second_len < 1024:
            second_len = 1024
        if second_len == main_len:
            second_len = 0

    polish_budget = max(20.0, 0.17 * usable)
    explore_budget = max(30.0, usable - polish_budget - 20.0)

    main_inits = _generate_inits(main_len, rng, provided_use, many=True)
    main_scored = _score_inits(main_inits, main_len)

    second_scored = []
    if second_len > 0:
        sec_inits = _generate_inits(second_len, rng, None, many=False)
        second_scored = _score_inits(sec_inits, second_len)

    tasks = []
    if main_scored:
        tasks.append((0.36, main_scored[0][1], "mirror", True))
        tasks.append((0.12, None, "adam", False))
        if len(main_scored) > 1:
            tasks.append((0.14, main_scored[1][1], "mirror", True))
        if len(main_scored) > 2:
            tasks.append((0.08, main_scored[2][1], "mirror", True))
    if second_scored:
        tasks.append((0.14, second_scored[0][1], "mirror", True))
        if len(second_scored) > 1:
            tasks.append((0.06, second_scored[1][1], "mirror", True))

    total_frac = float(sum(f for f, _, _, _ in tasks))
    if total_frac <= 0.0:
        tasks = [(1.0, np.ones(main_len, dtype=np.float64), "mirror", True)]
        total_frac = 1.0

    global_score = float("inf")
    global_a = None
    best_main_score = float("inf")
    best_main_a = None
    results = []

    for frac, init, method, use_best in tasks:
        time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if time_left < 5.0:
            break

        task_time = (frac / total_frac) * explore_budget
        if task_time < 5.0:
            continue

        if use_best and global_a is not None:
            a_use = global_a
        else:
            if init is None:
                if main_scored:
                    a_use = main_scored[0][1]
                else:
                    a_use = np.ones(main_len, dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                a_use = init

        if method == "mirror":
            a_best, score = run_smooth_mirror(
                a_use,
                task_time,
                rng,
                beta_start=2.0,
                beta_max=800.0,
                step_init=0.15,
                allow_restart=True
            )
        else:
            a_best, score = run_log_adam(
                a_use,
                task_time,
                rng,
                beta_start=5.0,
                beta_max=1200.0,
                lr_init=0.30,
                allow_restart=True
            )

        results.append((score, a_best))
        if np.isfinite(score):
            if score < global_score:
                global_score = score
                global_a = a_best.copy()
            if a_best.size == main_len and score < best_main_score:
                best_main_score = score
                best_main_a = a_best.copy()

    time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if全球 is not None and time_left > 5.0:
        pass

    if global_a is not None and time_left > 5.0:
        a_pol, score_pol = run_smooth_mirror(
            global_a,
            time_left,
            rng,
            beta_start=40.0,
            beta_max=2000.0,
            step_init=0.05,
            allow_restart=False
        )
        results.append((score_pol, a_pol))
        if np.isfinite(score_pol) and score_pol < global_score:
            global_score = score_pol
            global_a = a_pol.copy()

    eval_candidates = []
    if global_a is not None:
        eval_candidates.append(np.asarray(global_a, dtype=np.float64))
    if best_main_a is not None:
        if global_a is None or best_main_a.size != global_a.size:
            eval_candidates.append(np.asarray(best_main_a, dtype=np.float64))

    best_eval_val = float("inf")
    best_eval_a = None
    for arr in eval_candidates:
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size > 20000:
            continue
        if arr.size == 0 or arr.sum() < 0.01:
            continue
        try:
            val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr])
        except Exception:
            val = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(val):
            if best_eval_a is None:
                best_eval_val = val
                best_eval_a = arr
            elif val < best_eval_val - 1e-12:
                best_eval_val = val
                best_eval_a = arr
            elif abs(val - best_eval_val) <= 1e-12 and arr.size < len(best_eval_a):
                best_eval_val = val
                best_eval_a = arr

    if best_eval_a is not None:
        final_a = best_eval_a
    else:
        if global_a is not None:
            final_a = np.asarray(global_a, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            final_a = np.ones(main_len, dtype=np.float64)
        final_a = np.clip(final_a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if final_a.size == 0 or final_a.sum() < 0.01:
        final_a = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)

    print("final best fast score=%.7f" % (global_score if np.isfinite(global_score) else float("inf")))
    print("length returned=%d elapsed=%.2f" % (len(final_a), time.monotonic() - t0))

    return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
